## 0. WHAT PASS 1 SETTLED (fable5_p1b.md, claude-fable-5-1; re-inlined so nobody re-derives it)

* **The fix:** the PORT arm REFUSES a selector >= 8 on the full 64-bit `idata`, keeps the previous (always-valid)
  selector, and announces the refusal once per device with the house `(once)` marker.  Unanimous across nine seats.
* **The DATA arm's range check and `exit(1)` are KEPT, unedited except for a comment**, as a tripwire: once PORT refuses
  it is unreachable, and it is what stands between a future broken PORT arm and `port[8]` (which, after this diff's
  struct change, is the `warned_port` latch).  Precedent: `#521` left an unreachable `default:` exit "deliberately ... as
  a tripwire".  Dissent (codex panel, pass 1): make it a quiet refusal -- ruled against on the arguments.
* **Out of scope and FILED, not taken:** `fbctrl_command()`'s `default:` (any command other than 0-2, including the
  documented but unimplemented FILL (3) / COPY (4)) and the access function's `default:` (any offset other than 0x00 /
  0x10).  Both still end the process.  The Opus seat MEASURED that keep-previous-from-reset routes "refused selector,
  then a DATA write of an unknown command" into the command default; the contract says so.  Dissent (kimi-sub): the
  command default meets the reopening rule -- ruled against: a different fix design (implement vs decline FILL/COPY).
* **The rung-3 witness is valid** (unmodified `-E testmips`, stepped real `sw`/`lw`, liveness + device-signature +
  disassembly controls, death AND diagnostic required).  On HEAD: `sw 9` to PORT then `lw` from DATA ends the process,
  exit status 1.

+def hx(v):
+    return "-" if v is None else "0x%x" % v

+def lw32(v):
+    """what `lw` of a 32-bit device answer yields in a 64-bit register (sign-extended)"""
+    v &= 0xffffffff
+    return v | 0xffffffff00000000 if v & 0x80000000 else v

## 4. QUESTIONS

1. **Premise audit of the DIFF.**  Check every sentence the diff adds -- the two comments in `dev_fbctrl.c`, the probe's
   header (SESSIONS / CONTRACT / NOT CLAIMED), the gate-16 block's comment -- against the source and against section 1's
   measurements.  A sentence true only for the case measured, or overstating what a row observes, is a finding.  Pass 1
   found five such sentences in my own brief.
2. **What is the SMALLEST EDIT to `dev_fbctrl.c` -- outside the 25 variants in table 2 -- that breaks the refusal and still
   scores 41/41?**  Name the row you expect to stay green.  And the smallest edit to the PROBE or to the gate-16 block that
   keeps every pin green while the device is wrong.
3. **The gate-16 block.**  Is the parse guard complete (a missing, duplicated or malformed RESULT/CONTROL/DONE line; a
   probe that dies early)?  Does moving the pmax-kernel check below the block change gate 16's result for any state it
   could be in before (binary missing, kernel missing, fold rows red)?
4. **The contract.**  Is the NOT CLAIMED list complete and true -- the process-global latch, the deleted DATA check, the
   struct-layout sentence about `port[8]`, the command and offset defaults?  Is anything CLAIMED that no row observes?
5. **Records owed by this commit** (CHANGELOG block, OUTSTANDING_BUGS filings for the command default and the offset
   default, the ledger row) -- is anything owed that I have not listed?

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fbctrl.c
   1: /*
   2:  *  Copyright (C) 2006-2018  Anders Gavare.  All rights reserved.

  25:  *  SUCH DAMAGE.
  26:  *   
  27:  *
  28:  *  COMMENT: Framebuffer controller device (control's dev_fb in test machines)
  29:  *
  30:  *  A "framebuffer control" device. It can be used to manipulate the
  31:  *  framebuffer device in testmachines.
  32:  */
  33: 
  34: #include <stdio.h>
  35: #include <stdlib.h>
  36: #include <string.h>
  37: 
  38: #include "cpu.h"
  39: #include "device.h"
  40: #include "devices.h"
  41: #include "machine.h"
  42: #include "memory.h"
  43: #include "misc.h"
  44: 
  45: #include "testmachine/dev_fb.h"
  46: 
  47: 
  48: struct fbctrl_data {
  49: 	struct vfb_data *vfb_data;
  50: 	int		current_port;
  51: 	int		port[DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS];
  52: 	int		warned_port;	/* #549: the refused-selector announce */
  53: };
  54: 
  55: 
  56: /*
  57:  *  fbctrl_command():
  58:  *
  59:  *  Execute a framebuffer control command.
  60:  */
  61: static void fbctrl_command(struct cpu *cpu, struct fbctrl_data *d)
  62: {
  63: 	int cmd = d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_COMMAND];
  64: 	int x1, y1, i;
  65: 
  66: 	switch (cmd) {
  67: 
  68: 	case DEV_FBCTRL_COMMAND_NOP:
  69: 		break;
  70: 
  71: 	case DEV_FBCTRL_COMMAND_SET_RESOLUTION:
  72: 		/*
  73: 		 *  Change the framebuffer resolution to X1 x Y1 pixels.
  74: 		 */
  75: 		x1 = d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_X1];
  76: 		y1 = d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_Y1];
  77: 		debug("[ dev_fbctrl: changing resolution to %i,%i ]\n", x1, y1);
  78: 
  79: 		dev_fb_resize(d->vfb_data, x1, y1);
  80: 
  81: 		/*  Remember to invalidate all translations for anyone
  82: 		    who might have used the old framebuffer:  */
  83: 		for (i = 0; i < cpu->machine->ncpus; i++)
  84: 			cpu->machine->cpus[i]->invalidate_translation_caches(
  85: 			    cpu->machine->cpus[i], 0, INVALIDATE_ALL);
  86: 		break;
  87: 
  88: 	case DEV_FBCTRL_COMMAND_GET_RESOLUTION:
  89: 		if (cpu->machine->x11_md.in_use) {
  90: 			d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_X1] = d->vfb_data->xsize;
  91: 			d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_Y1] = d->vfb_data->ysize;
  92: 		} else {
  93: 			// When not using X11 output, return 0x0 size.
  94: 			d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_X1] = 0;
  95: 			d->port[DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_Y1] = 0;
  96: 		}
  97: 		break;
  98: 
  99: 	/*  TODO: Block copy and fill.  */
 100: 
 101: 	default:fatal("fbctrl_command: Unimplemented command %i.\n", cmd);
 102: 		exit(1);
 103: 	}
 104: }
 105: 
 106: 
 107: DEVICE_ACCESS(fbctrl)
 108: {
 109: 	struct fbctrl_data *d = (struct fbctrl_data *) extra;
 110: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 111: 
 112: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 113: 	        idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 114: 
 115: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 116: 
 117: 	case DEV_FBCTRL_PORT:
 118: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 119: 			odata = d->current_port;
 120: 		else if (idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {
 121: 			/*  #549: an out-of-range selector is REFUSED, and
 122: 			    current_port keeps its previous, always-valid value.
 123: 			    It used to be stored with only a warning, and the
 124: 			    next DATA access then ended the emulator in the
 125: 			    range check below.  Announced once per device.  */
 126: 			if (!d->warned_port) {
 127: 				fatal("[ fbctrl: port number 0x%llx is out of range; "
 128: 				    "the selector is unchanged (once) ]\n",
 129: 				    (unsigned long long) idata);
 130: 				d->warned_port = 1;
 131: 			}
 132: 		} else
 133: 			d->current_port = idata;
 134: 		break;
 135: 
 136: 	case DEV_FBCTRL_DATA:
 137: 		/*  #549: unreachable while the PORT arm refuses; kept as a
 138: 		    tripwire for a later edit that lets a bad selector in.  */
 139: 		if (d->current_port < 0 || d->current_port
 140: 		    >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {
 141: 			fatal("[ fbctrl port number is out of range! ]\n");
 142: 			exit(1);
 143: 		}
 144: 
 145: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 146: 			odata = d->port[d->current_port];
 147: 		else {
 148: 			d->port[d->current_port] = idata;
 149: 			if (d->current_port == DEV_FBCTRL_PORT_COMMAND)
 150: 				fbctrl_command(cpu, d);
 151: 		}
 152: 
 153: 		break;
 154: 
 155: 	default:
 156: 		fatal("[ dev_fbctrl: unimplemented relative addr 0x%x ]\n",
 157: 		    (int)relative_addr);
 158: 		exit(1);
 159: 	}
 160: 
 161: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 162: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 163: 
 164: 	return 1;
 165: }
 166: 
 167: 
 168: DEVINIT(fbctrl)
 169: {
 170: 	struct fbctrl_data *d;
 171: 
 172: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct fbctrl_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct fbctrl_data)));
 173: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct fbctrl_data));
 174: 
 175: 	d->vfb_data = dev_fb_init(devinit->machine, devinit->machine->memory,
 176: 	    DEV_FB_ADDRESS, VFB_GENERIC, 640, 480, 640, DEV_FBCTRL_MAXY(640),
 177: 	    24, "generic");
 178: 
 179: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
 180: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FBCTRL_LENGTH, dev_fbctrl_access, d,
 181: 	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 182: 
 183: 	return 1;
 184: }
 185: 
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: r"""#549 fbctrlportkill -- the DETECTOR for dev_fbctrl.c's port selector, on an UNMODIFIED `-E testmips`.
   3: 
   4: machine_test.c installs `fbctrl addr=0x12f00000` in testmips's own setup (and in default_test() for the other test
   5: machines).  Every access below is a real guest sb/sh/sw/sd/lw placed with `put w` and executed through real address decode
   6: and memory_rw -- stepped one instruction at a time, except session F, which free-runs to a breakpoint.  No machine or
   7: device is added to reach the site.  t0 = 0xffffffffb2f00000, kseg1 of DEV_FBCTRL_ADDRESS; PORT is +0x00, DATA +0x10.
   8: 
   9: THE DEFECT (HEAD before #549): the PORT arm STORED any selector with only a warning, and the DATA arm then called exit(1)
  10: on the stored int -- `sw 9` to PORT followed by any DATA access ended the emulator process.  #549 REFUSES a selector >= 8
  11: at the PORT arm (the previous, valid selector is kept) and announces the refusal once per device, `(once)`.
  12: 
  13: SESSIONS (each on a fresh emulator)
  14:   C  controls: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, read both back (LIVENESS); X1=123, COMMAND=GET_RESOLUTION, read X1 (DEVICE SIGNATURE:
  15:      the model zeroes X1/Y1 without X11 -- RAM at +0x10 would read back 2, the last value written there, and a banked
  16:      register WITHOUT command execution would read 123); no `(once)` line may appear (nothing is refused).
  17:   A  accept: all EIGHT selectors, written at widths 1, 2, 4 and 8, each read back through PORT; ports 1-7 round-trip
  18:      a distinct DATA value (port 0 is COMMAND and is written only with NOP).
  19:   P  refusal, anchored to a previous selector: PORT=5 (DATA 0x2a), then nine out-of-range writes -- 9, 8, 16 (sw),
  20:      0x200000000, 0x100000003, all-ones (sd), all-ones (sw), 0xff (sb), 0x100 (sh) -- each PRECEDED by a fresh
  21:      PORT=5 and followed by a PORT read that must still say 5, so every row fails only on its own value; then
  22:      PORT=7 and a refused 9 must leave 7.  The `(once)` count must be exactly 1 right
  23:      after the FIRST refusal and still 1 after the last.
  24:   K  the witness pair, repaired: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, PORT=9 refused, then a DATA READ (must survive and read port 5's
  25:      0x2a) and a DATA WRITE of 0x77 (must land in port 5: read back through PORT=5).
  26:   F  free-running: one refused PORT write, then `continue` to a breakpoint.  Stepping raises debug()'s verbosity, so a
  27:      refusal announced with debug() instead of fatal() would pass every stepped row; free-running it does not print.
  28: 
  29: CONTRACT (clause e).  CATCHES: the store reached for an out-of-range value (or a clamped/masked/reset one), the compare
  30: narrowed or signed (bit 63, bits 32-33, 32-bit truncation), a boundary slip at 8, a refusal applied to some widths only,
  31: a legal selector refused, a refusal that rewrites the previous selector, a latch never set / set late / keyed per value,
  32: an announce demoted to debug(), and the pre-#549 process exit on DATA (kill table 1: 20 of 20 such edits red,
  33: regress/artifacts/fbctrlportkill_20260924/table1/TABLE.out).  NOT CLAIMED: a process-global latch instead of the
  34: per-instance field (every machine installs ONE fbctrl; adding a device to reach a second would be laundering); the
  35: deletion of the DATA arm's range check, which a correct PORT arm makes unreachable -- it is KEPT as a tripwire, and it
  36: matters beyond style: `warned_port` sits right after `port[8]`, so an index of 8 that got past a broken PORT arm would
  37: land on the latch itself; and the device's OTHER two process-ending calls -- fbctrl_command()'s default (any command
  38: other than 0-2, including the documented but unimplemented FILL/COPY; a refused selector at power-on leaves COMMAND
  39: selected, so a following DATA write of such a value reaches it) and the access function's default (any offset other
  40: than 0x00/0x10).  Those are filed.  Session P re-selects PORT=5 before each refusal: kill table 1 ran without the
  41: re-select, and one corrupted selector then turned every later "keeps 5" row red, overstating which row catches
  42: what (the flagship seat, pass 1).
  43: 
  44: Every stepped word is compared with the emulator's own step line (the hand-assembled-encoding trap).
  45: Output: one row per check, then FBCTRLP_CONTROL=OK|FAILED, FBCTRLP_RESULT=n/t, FBCTRLP_DONE.
  46: usage: fbctrl_port_probe.py <gxemul-binary>
  47: """
  48: import os
  49: import pty
  50: import re
  51: import select
  52: import signal
  53: import sys
  54: import time
  55: 
  56: BIN = sys.argv[1]
  57: 
  58: STUBADDR = 0xffffffff80100000
  59: CODE = 0xffffffff80100020
  60: FBCTRL = 0xffffffffb2f00000
  61: 
  62: ONCE_RE = re.compile(r"\[ fbctrl: port number 0x[0-9a-f]+ is out of range; the selector is unchanged \(once\) \]")
  63: 
  64: REG = dict(zr=0, v0=2, v1=3, a0=4, a1=5, a2=6, a3=7, t0=8, t1=9, t2=10, t3=11, t4=12, t5=13, t6=14, t7=15,
  65:            s0=16, s1=17, s2=18, s3=19, s4=20, s5=21, s6=22, s7=23, t8=24, t9=25)
  66: OPS = dict(sb=0x28, sh=0x29, sw=0x2b, sd=0x3f, lw=0x23)
  67: 
  68: 
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_mips_folds.sh
   1: #!/bin/bash
   2: # GATE 16 -- MIPS instruction-combiner ("fold") witness (#388).
   3: #
   4: # What this protects
   5: # -----------------
   6: # The nine MIPS COMBINE sites install 34 fold-handler variants, and before #388
   7: # NOTHING could witness one firing: most folds are architecturally transparent
   8: # by construction, so any result-asserting row passes whether or not the fold
   9: # fires -- the recorded vacuity mode. #388 added pull-only install/fire counters
  10: # (cpu_mips.h) printed by tlbdump as a prefix-bound MFOLD block. This gate runs
  11: # regress/mips_fold_probe.py, whose rows drive real fold loops free-running
  12: # (never step -- combining gates on !single_step) with a breakpoint AFTER each
  13: # sequence on a NON-ARMING instruction, and grades the counters as EQUALITIES
  14: # derived from the read-ahead rule (a breakpoint anywhere disables read-ahead
  15: # machine-wide, so fire == passes-1; the derivations live in the probe's rows).
  16: #
  17: # Non-vacuity: deleting the bne_samepage_nop replacement sub-arm (incl. its
  18: # install++) in a scratch build flips exactly that fold to (0,0) and reddens
  19: # its two rows while every other row stays green -- measured 2026-08-12
  20: # (M388MUT_PASS), the per-variant attribution -J cannot give.
  21: #
  22: # The BE rows are the first big-endian MIPS guest executions in this harness;
  23: # the mlw2v* value rows additionally witness the _be generated body's BE32
  24: # assembly + sign extension, not just its selection.
  25: set -u
  26: cd "$(dirname "$0")"
  27: . ./lib.sh
  28: 
  29: gate_begin "gate 16: MIPS fold witness (#388)"
  30: 
  31: PMAX=${PMAX:-$ROOT/build/gxemul}
  32: PMAX_KERNEL=$ROOT/gxemul_pmax_rig/bsd
  33: 
  34: if [ ! -x "$PMAX" ]; then gate_skip "no gxemul binary at $PMAX"; fi
  35: 
  36: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  37: #  #549 fbctrlportkill: dev_fbctrl.c's PORT selector, on -E testmips.  Before #549 a guest
  38: #  store of 8 or more to PORT was STORED with only a warning, and the next DATA access ended
  39: #  the emulator in the DATA arm's range check.  #549 refuses the selector (the previous one
  40: #  is kept) and announces the refusal once.  regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py drives real guest
  41: #  sb/sh/sw/sd/lw on an unmodified testmips -- no kernel, no rig image -- so it runs BEFORE
  42: #  this gate's pmax-kernel check: a missing kernel still skips the fold rows below, and
  43: #  gate_skip reports FAIL, not SKIP, if an fbctrl row failed first.
  44: #
  45: #  THE CONTRACT is the probe's header (CATCHES / NOT CLAIMED).  Kill table 1 was red on 20 of
  46: #  20 edits of the fix's own predicate, store and latch; a process-global latch and the
  47: #  deletion of the DATA arm's kept check are DISCLOSED escapes; and the device still ends the
  48: #  process through its command and offset defaults, which are filed, not claimed.  HEAD before
  49: #  #549 scored 25/41 here, every refusal row red.
  50: #
  51: #  `gatedevicesplit` is OWED (#498): one more device probe in a CPU gate, as #548 added to
  52: #  gate 14.  Recorded, not compounded silently.

  54: fbrc=0

  56: if [ "$(grep -cx 'FBCTRLP_DONE' "$FBCLOG")" != "1" ] ||

  64: else
  65:     check "fbctrl probe output parses" "yes" "yes"
  66:     grep -E '^  FAIL ' "$FBCLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
  67:     check "fbctrl probe controls" "$(sed -n 's/^FBCTRLP_CONTROL=//p' "$FBCLOG")" "OK"
  68:     check "fbctrl probe exit status" "$fbrc" "0"
  69:     fbres=$(sed -n 's/^FBCTRLP_RESULT=//p' "$FBCLOG")
  70:     #  Pinned UNQUOTED under a one-token stem, so check_score_claims.py sees the pin.

  72:     check "fbctrl_port rows correct" "${fbres%/*}" "41"

### regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py (new file)

```diff
diff --git a/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py b/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..352c6d24
--- /dev/null
+++ b/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py
@@ -0,0 +1,366 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+r"""#549 fbctrlportkill -- the DETECTOR for dev_fbctrl.c's port selector, on an UNMODIFIED `-E testmips`.
+
+machine_test.c installs `fbctrl addr=0x12f00000` in testmips's own setup (and in default_test() for the other test
+machines).  Every access below is a real guest sb/sh/sw/sd/lw placed with `put w` and executed through real address decode
+and memory_rw -- stepped one instruction at a time, except session F, which free-runs to a breakpoint.  No machine or
+device is added to reach the site.  t0 = 0xffffffffb2f00000, kseg1 of DEV_FBCTRL_ADDRESS; PORT is +0x00, DATA +0x10.
+
+THE DEFECT (HEAD before #549): the PORT arm STORED any selector with only a warning, and the DATA arm then called exit(1)
+on the stored int -- `sw 9` to PORT followed by any DATA access ended the emulator process.  #549 REFUSES a selector >= 8
+at the PORT arm (the previous, valid selector is kept) and announces the refusal once per device, `(once)`.
+
+SESSIONS (each on a fresh emulator)
+  C  controls: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, read both back (LIVENESS); X1=123, COMMAND=GET_RESOLUTION, read X1 (DEVICE SIGNATURE:
+     the model zeroes X1/Y1 without X11 -- RAM at +0x10 would read back 2, the last value written there, and a banked
+     register WITHOUT command execution would read 123); no `(once)` line may appear (nothing is refused).
+  A  accept: all EIGHT selectors, written at widths 1, 2, 4 and 8, each read back through PORT; ports 1-7 round-trip
+     a distinct DATA value (port 0 is COMMAND and is written only with NOP).
+  P  refusal, anchored to a previous selector: PORT=5 (DATA 0x2a), then nine out-of-range writes -- 9, 8, 16 (sw),
+     0x200000000, 0x100000003, all-ones (sd), all-ones (sw), 0xff (sb), 0x100 (sh) -- each PRECEDED by a fresh
+     PORT=5 and followed by a PORT read that must still say 5, so every row fails only on its own value; then
+     PORT=7 and a refused 9 must leave 7.  The `(once)` count must be exactly 1 right
+     after the FIRST refusal and still 1 after the last.
+  K  the witness pair, repaired: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, PORT=9 refused, then a DATA READ (must survive and read port 5's
+     0x2a) and a DATA WRITE of 0x77 (must land in port 5: read back through PORT=5).
+  F  free-running: one refused PORT write, then `continue` to a breakpoint.  Stepping raises debug()'s verbosity, so a
+     refusal announced with debug() instead of fatal() would pass every stepped row; free-running it does not print.
+
+CONTRACT (clause e).  CATCHES: the store reached for an out-of-range value (or a clamped/masked/reset one), the compare
+narrowed or signed (bit 63, bits 32-33, 32-bit truncation), a boundary slip at 8, a refusal applied to some widths only,
+a legal selector refused, a refusal that rewrites the previous selector, a latch never set / set late / keyed per value,
+an announce demoted to debug(), and the pre-#549 process exit on DATA (kill table 1: 20 of 20 such edits red,
+regress/artifacts/fbctrlportkill_20260924/table1/TABLE.out).  NOT CLAIMED: a process-global latch instead of the
+per-instance field (every machine installs ONE fbctrl; adding a device to reach a second would be laundering); the
+deletion of the DATA arm's range check, which a correct PORT arm makes unreachable -- it is KEPT as a tripwire, and it
+matters beyond style: `warned_port` sits right after `port[8]`, so an index of 8 that got past a broken PORT arm would
+land on the latch itself; and the device's OTHER two process-ending calls -- fbctrl_command()'s default (any command
+other than 0-2, including the documented but unimplemented FILL/COPY; a refused selector at power-on leaves COMMAND
+selected, so a following DATA write of such a value reaches it) and the access function's default (any offset other
+than 0x00/0x10).  Those are filed.  Session P re-selects PORT=5 before each refusal: kill table 1 ran without the
+re-select, and one corrupted selector then turned every later "keeps 5" row red, overstating which row catches
+what (the flagship seat, pass 1).
+
+Every stepped word is compared with the emulator's own step line (the hand-assembled-encoding trap).
+Output: one row per check, then FBCTRLP_CONTROL=OK|FAILED, FBCTRLP_RESULT=n/t, FBCTRLP_DONE.
+usage: fbctrl_port_probe.py <gxemul-binary>
+"""
+import os
+import pty
+import re
+import select
+import signal
+import sys
+import time
+
+BIN = sys.argv[1]
+
+STUBADDR = 0xffffffff80100000
+CODE = 0xffffffff80100020
+FBCTRL = 0xffffffffb2f00000
+
+ONCE_RE = re.compile(r"\[ fbctrl: port number 0x[0-9a-f]+ is out of range; the selector is unchanged \(once\) \]")
+
+REG = dict(zr=0, v0=2, v1=3, a0=4, a1=5, a2=6, a3=7, t0=8, t1=9, t2=10, t3=11, t4=12, t5=13, t6=14, t7=15,
+           s0=16, s1=17, s2=18, s3=19, s4=20, s5=21, s6=22, s7=23, t8=24, t9=25)
+OPS = dict(sb=0x28, sh=0x29, sw=0x2b, sd=0x3f, lw=0x23)
+
+
+def ins(op, rt, off, base="t0"):
+    return (OPS[op] << 26) | (REG[base] << 21) | (REG[rt] << 16) | (off & 0xffff)

codex
The added comments overstate the old behavior: a wide selector could truncate to a valid stored `int`, so an out-of-range write did not always make the next DATA access terminate. I’m also checking whether the probe’s controls and gate parser support their claims.

1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:r"""#549 fbctrlportkill -- the DETECTOR for dev_fbctrl.c's port selector, on an UNMODIFIED `-E testmips`.
3:
4:machine_test.c installs `fbctrl addr=0x12f00000` in testmips's own setup (and in default_test() for the other test
5:machines).  Every access below is a real guest sb/sh/sw/sd/lw placed with `put w` and executed through real address decode
6:and memory_rw -- stepped one instruction at a time, except session F, which free-runs to a breakpoint.  No machine or
7:device is added to reach the site.  t0 = 0xffffffffb2f00000, kseg1 of DEV_FBCTRL_ADDRESS; PORT is +0x00, DATA +0x10.
8:
9:THE DEFECT (HEAD before #549): the PORT arm STORED any selector with only a warning, and the DATA arm then called exit(1)
10:on the stored int -- `sw 9` to PORT followed by any DATA access ended the emulator process.  #549 REFUSES a selector >= 8
11:at the PORT arm (the previous, valid selector is kept) and announces the refusal once per device, `(once)`.
12:
13:SESSIONS (each on a fresh emulator)
14:  C  controls: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, read both back (LIVENESS); X1=123, COMMAND=GET_RESOLUTION, read X1 (DEVICE SIGNATURE:
15:     the model zeroes X1/Y1 without X11 -- RAM at +0x10 would read back 2, the last value written there, and a banked
16:     register WITHOUT command execution would read 123); no `(once)` line may appear (nothing is refused).
17:  A  accept: all EIGHT selectors, written at widths 1, 2, 4 and 8, each read back through PORT; ports 1-7 round-trip
18:     a distinct DATA value (port 0 is COMMAND and is written only with NOP).
19:  P  refusal, anchored to a previous selector: PORT=5 (DATA 0x2a), then nine out-of-range writes -- 9, 8, 16 (sw),
20:     0x200000000, 0x100000003, all-ones (sd), all-ones (sw), 0xff (sb), 0x100 (sh) -- each PRECEDED by a fresh
21:     PORT=5 and followed by a PORT read that must still say 5, so every row fails only on its own value; then
22:     PORT=7 and a refused 9 must leave 7.  The `(once)` count must be exactly 1 right
23:     after the FIRST refusal and still 1 after the last.
24:  K  the witness pair, repaired: PORT=5, DATA=0x2a, PORT=9 refused, then a DATA READ (must survive and read port 5's
25:     0x2a) and a DATA WRITE of 0x77 (must land in port 5: read back through PORT=5).
26:  F  free-running: one refused PORT write, then `continue` to a breakpoint.  Stepping raises debug()'s verbosity, so a
27:     refusal announced with debug() instead of fatal() would pass every stepped row; free-running it does not print.
28:
29:CONTRACT (clause e).  CATCHES: the store reached for an out-of-range value (or a clamped/masked/reset one), the compare
30:narrowed or signed (bit 63, bits 32-33, 32-bit truncation), a boundary slip at 8, a refusal applied to some widths only,
31:a legal selector refused, a refusal that rewrites the previous selector, a latch never set / set late / keyed per value,
32:an announce demoted to debug(), and the pre-#549 process exit on DATA (kill table 1: 20 of 20 such edits red,
33:regress/artifacts/fbctrlportkill_20260924/table1/TABLE.out).  NOT CLAIMED: a process-global latch instead of the
34:per-instance field (every machine installs ONE fbctrl; adding a device to reach a second would be laundering); the
35:deletion of the DATA arm's range check, which a correct PORT arm makes unreachable -- it is KEPT as a tripwire, and it
36:matters beyond style: `warned_port` sits right after `port[8]`, so an index of 8 that got past a broken PORT arm would
37:land on the latch itself; and the device's OTHER two process-ending calls -- fbctrl_command()'s default (any command
38:other than 0-2, including the documented but unimplemented FILL/COPY; a refused selector at power-on leaves COMMAND
39:selected, so a following DATA write of such a value reaches it) and the access function's default (any offset other
40:than 0x00/0x10).  Those are filed.  Session P re-selects PORT=5 before each refusal: kill table 1 ran without the
41:re-select, and one corrupted selector then turned every later "keeps 5" row red, overstating which row catches
42:what (the flagship seat, pass 1).
43:
44:Every stepped word is compared with the emulator's own step line (the hand-assembled-encoding trap).
45:Output: one row per check, then FBCTRLP_CONTROL=OK|FAILED, FBCTRLP_RESULT=n/t, FBCTRLP_DONE.
46:usage: fbctrl_port_probe.py <gxemul-binary>

58:STUBADDR = 0xffffffff80100000
59:CODE = 0xffffffff80100020
60:FBCTRL = 0xffffffffb2f00000
61:
62:ONCE_RE = re.compile(r"\[ fbctrl: port number 0x[0-9a-f]+ is out of range; the selector is unchanged \(once\) \]")
63:
64:REG = dict(zr=0, v0=2, v1=3, a0=4, a1=5, a2=6, a3=7, t0=8, t1=9, t2=10, t3=11, t4=12, t5=13, t6=14, t7=15,
65:           s0=16, s1=17, s2=18, s3=19, s4=20, s5=21, s6=22, s7=23, t8=24, t9=25)
66:OPS = dict(sb=0x28, sh=0x29, sw=0x2b, sd=0x3f, lw=0x23)

69:def ins(op, rt, off, base="t0"):
70:    return (OPS[op] << 26) | (REG[base] << 21) | (REG[rt] << 16) | (off & 0xffff)
71:
72:
73:def text(op, rt, off, base="t0"):
74:    return "%s %s,%d(%s)" % (op, rt, off, base)
75:
76:
77:ANCHOR = (0x0f << 26) | (REG["t7"] << 16)                   # lui t7,0 -- the breakpoint anchor, never executed
78:#  Pinned independently of the helper: a wrong register field would otherwise pass silently.
79:assert ins("sw", "t1", 0) == 0xad090000
80:assert ins("lw", "t2", 0x10) == 0x8d0a0010
81:assert ins("sd", "t5", 0) == 0xfd0d0000
82:assert ins("sb", "t8", 0) == 0xa1180000
83:assert ins("sh", "t9", 0) == 0xa5190000
84:assert ANCHOR == 0x3c0f0000

87:def stub_path():
88:    p = "/tmp/gx_fbctrl549p_%d.bin" % os.getpid()
89:    with open(p, "wb") as f:
90:        f.write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")
91:    return p

94:def session(label, prog, regs, reads, stub, free_run=False):
95:    """prog: list of (op, rt, off, tag).  Steps each (or free-runs to an anchor after the last), then prints reads.
96:    Returns dict: status, exit, badis, vals, once_after (tag -> cumulative (once) count right after that step), buf."""
97:    pid, fd = pty.fork()
98:    if pid == 0:
99:        os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-E", "testmips", "-M", "64", "0x%x:%s" % (STUBADDR, stub)])
100:        os._exit(127)
101:    buf = ""
102:    dead = [False]
103:
104:    def rd(t=0.4):
105:        nonlocal buf
106:        r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
107:        if fd not in r:
108:            return True
109:        try:
110:            d = os.read(fd, 65536)
111:        except OSError:
112:            dead[0] = True
113:            return False
114:        if not d:
115:            dead[0] = True
116:            return False
117:        buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
118:        return True
119:
120:    def wait(mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
121:        t = time.time()
122:        while time.time() - t < timeout:
123:            if not rd():
124:                return False
125:            resp = buf[mark:]
126:            if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
127:                continue
128:            if len(buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
129:                return True
130:        return False
131:
132:    def send(x, timeout=60):
133:        b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
134:        _mark = len(buf)
135:        n = 0
136:        try:
137:            while n < len(b):
138:                n += os.write(fd, b[n:])
139:        except OSError:
140:            dead[0] = True
141:            return False
142:        return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
143:
144:    words = [ins(op, rt, off) for op, rt, off, _t in prog] + [ANCHOR]
145:    status, badis, vals, once_after, steps = "?", [], {}, {}, 0
146:    if not wait(timeout=120):
147:        status = "NO-PROMPT"
148:    else:
149:        for i, w in enumerate(words):
150:            send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, w))
151:        for name, val in regs:
152:            send("%s=0x%x" % (name, val & 0xffffffffffffffff))
153:        send("pc=0x%x" % CODE)
154:        status = "SURVIVED"
155:        if free_run:
156:            send("breakpoint add 0x%x" % (CODE + 4 * len(prog)))
157:            mark = len(buf)
158:            b = b"continue\n"
159:            try:
160:                os.write(fd, b)
161:            except OSError:
162:                dead[0] = True
163:            if not wait(mark=mark, timeout=60) or dead[0]:
164:                status = "HOST-DIED" if dead[0] else "NO-BREAK"
165:            elif ("%016x" % (CODE + 4 * len(prog))) not in buf[mark:] and \
166:                    ("%08x" % ((CODE + 4 * len(prog)) & 0xffffffff)) not in buf[mark:]:
167:                status = "NO-BREAK"
168:            else:
169:                steps = len(prog)
170:                once_after["free"] = len(ONCE_RE.findall(buf))
171:        else:
172:            for i, (op, rt, off, tag) in enumerate(prog):
173:                mark = len(buf)
174:                if not send("step 1", timeout=30) or dead[0]:
175:                    status = "HOST-DIED"
176:                    break
177:                steps += 1
178:                seg = buf[mark:]
179:                w = words[i]
180:                m = re.search(r"%016x:?\s+%08x\s+([^\r\n<]+)" % (CODE + 4 * i, w), seg)
181:                got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(1)).strip() if m else "(no step line)"
182:                if not got.startswith(text(op, rt, off)):
183:                    badis.append("+0x%02x planted 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r" % (4 * i, w, got, text(op, rt, off)))
184:                if tag:
185:                    once_after[tag] = len(ONCE_RE.findall(buf))
186:        if status == "SURVIVED":
187:            for name in reads:
188:                got = None
189:                for _ in range(2):
190:                    mark = len(buf)
191:                    if not send("print %s" % name, timeout=30) or dead[0]:
192:                        break
193:                    m = re.search(r"(?m)^0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)\s*$", buf[mark:])
194:                    if m:
195:                        got = int(m.group(1), 16)
196:                        break
197:                vals[name] = got
198:    try:
199:        os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
200:    except OSError:
201:        pass
202:    t1 = time.time()
203:    while time.time() - t1 < 20.0:
204:        if not rd(0.1):
205:            break
206:    try:
207:        os.close(fd)
208:    except OSError:
209:        pass
210:    rpid, st = 0, None
211:    t1 = time.time()
212:    while rpid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
213:        try:
214:            rpid, st = os.waitpid(pid, os.WNOHANG)
215:        except OSError:
216:            break
217:        if rpid == 0:
218:            time.sleep(0.05)
219:    if rpid == 0:
220:        try:
221:            os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)
222:            _, st = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
223:        except OSError:
224:            st = None
225:    code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
226:    print("  session %-2s %-9s steps=%d/%d exit=%s once=%d" % (label, status, steps, len(prog), code,
227:                                                              len(ONCE_RE.findall(buf))))
228:    for b in badis:
229:        print("      DISASSEMBLY MISMATCH: %s" % b)
230:    return {"status": status, "exit": code, "badis": badis, "vals": vals, "once_after": once_after,
231:            "steps": steps, "buf": buf, "n": len(prog)}

237:def row(name, got, want, note=""):
238:    ok = got == want
239:    RESULTS.append(ok)
240:    print("  %-4s %-48s got=%-20s want=%-20s %s" % ("ok" if ok else "FAIL", name, got, want, note))

244:def hx(v):
245:    return "-" if v is None else "0x%x" % v

248:M64 = 0xffffffffffffffff

251:def lw32(v):
252:    """what `lw` of a 32-bit device answer yields in a 64-bit register (sign-extended)"""
253:    v &= 0xffffffff
254:    return v | 0xffffffff00000000 if v & 0x80000000 else v

267:#  Port 0 is COMMAND: written with NOP (0, from zr) only.
268:ACC = [(0, "sw"), (1, "sb"), (2, "sh"), (3, "sw"), (4, "sd"), (5, "sb"), (6, "sh"), (7, "sd")]
269:SEL_REG = ["t1", "t2", "t3", "t4", "t5", "t6", "t7", "t8"]           # the selector values
270:VAL_REG = ["zr", "a0", "a1", "a2", "a3", "v0", "v1", "t9"]           # the DATA values (port 0 gets zr = NOP)
271:PORT_RB = ["s0", "s1", "s2", "s3", "s4", "s5", "s6", "s7"]           # PORT read-backs
272:PROG_A, REGS_A = [], [("t0", FBCTRL)]
273:for k, (p, w) in enumerate(ACC):
274:    REGS_A.append((SEL_REG[k], p))
275:    REGS_A.append((PORT_RB[k], 0xdead))
276:    if k:
277:        REGS_A.append((VAL_REG[k], 0x11 * p))
278:    PROG_A.append((w, SEL_REG[k], 0, None))                            # PORT = p at width w
279:    PROG_A.append(("lw", PORT_RB[k], 0, None))                         # read PORT
280:    PROG_A.append(("sw", VAL_REG[k], 16, None))                        # DATA = 0x11*p (NOP for port 0)
281:#  the DATA read-backs, one per port 1..7: re-select p, read DATA into the selector register
282:for k, (p, w) in enumerate(ACC):
283:    if k:
284:        PROG_A.append(("sw", SEL_REG[k], 0, None))
285:        PROG_A.append(("lw", SEL_REG[k], 16, None))
286:READS_A = PORT_RB + SEL_REG[1:]
287:
288:#  P: previous selector 5, then nine refusals, then previous 7 and one more.
289:REFUSE = [("sw", "t1", 9, "9 (sw)"), ("sw", "t2", 8, "8 (sw)"), ("sw", "t3", 16, "16 (sw)"),
290:          ("sd", "t4", 0x200000000, "0x200000000 (sd)"), ("sd", "t5", 0x100000003, "0x100000003 (sd)"),
291:          ("sd", "t6", M64, "all-ones (sd)"), ("sw", "t7", 0xffffffff, "all-ones (sw)"),
292:          ("sb", "t8", 0xff, "0xff (sb)"), ("sh", "t9", 0x100, "0x100 (sh)")]
293:READ_P = ["s0", "s1", "s2", "s3", "s4", "s5", "s6", "s7", "v0"]
294:PROG_P = [("sw", "a0", 0, None), ("sw", "a1", 16, None)]
295:REGS_P = [("t0", FBCTRL), ("a0", 5), ("a1", 0x2a), ("a2", 7)]
296:for k, (w, r, v, _n) in enumerate(REFUSE):

300:    PROG_P.append((w, r, 0, "r%d" % k))

317:        A = session("A", PROG_A, REGS_A, READS_A, stub)
318:        P = session("P", PROG_P, REGS_P, READS_P, stub)

320:        F = session("F", PROG_F, REGS_F, [], stub, free_run=True)

329:                   "SURVIVED %d/%d" % (len(PROG_C), len(PROG_C))))
330:    ctl.append(row("C disassembly of every stepped word", len(C["badis"]), 0))
331:    ctl.append(row("C LIVENESS: DATA round-trips 0x2a", hx(C["vals"].get("t4")), "0x2a"))

336:    for S in (A, P, K):
337:        ctl.append(row("%s disassembly of every stepped word" % "APK"[(A, P, K).index(S)], len(S["badis"]), 0))
338:    print("ACCEPT (session A: %s %d/%d)" % (A["status"], A["steps"], A["n"]))
339:    for k, (p, w) in enumerate(ACC):

342:        if k:
343:            row("port %d round-trips its DATA value" % p, hx(A["vals"].get(SEL_REG[k])), hx(0x11 * p))

345:    for k, (w, r, v, name) in enumerate(REFUSE):
346:        row("refuse %s, PORT keeps 5" % name, hx(P["vals"].get(READ_P[k])), "0x5")

349:    row("announce: still exactly 1 after ten refusals", P["once_after"].get("r9"), 1)
350:    print("THE WITNESS PAIR (session K)")
351:    row("K survives DATA after a refused PORT write", "%s %d/%d" % (K["status"], K["steps"], K["n"]),

355:    print("FREE-RUNNING (session F)")

357:    row("F refusal announced while free-running (fatal)", F["once_after"].get("free"), 1)
358:    control = all(ctl)
359:    n_ok, n = sum(RESULTS), len(RESULTS)
360:    print("FBCTRLP_CONTROL=%s" % ("OK" if control else "FAILED"))
361:    print("FBCTRLP_RESULT=%d/%d" % (n_ok, n))

1:#!/bin/bash
2:# Shared helpers for the regression gates.
3:#
4:# Two rules encoded here, both learned by getting them wrong:
5:#
6:#  1. RUN UNBUFFERED. When gxemul's stdout is a pipe, libc switches to 4 KB block
7:#     buffering. `timeout` then kills it with SIGTERM and the partial block is lost, so a
8:#     guest that produced 3 KB of perfectly good boot output scores ZERO bytes while one
9:#     that produced 5 KB scores 4096. An early version of this harness compared those
10:#     numbers across builds and "found" a capability regression that was pure buffering.
11:#     Every emulator invocation goes through run_emu() or run_emu_progress(), both of which
12:#     force stdbuf -o0 -e0.  (#419 note: run_emu() itself now has NO CALLERS -- gate 7 was
13:#     its last one.  It is kept because the rule is general and a future gate will want the
14:#     simple form, but do not cite it as the universal path: run_emu_progress() is.)
15:#
16:#  2. A MISSING INPUT IS A HARD ERROR, NEVER A ZERO SCORE. The same early version had no
17:#     existence check on the baseline binaries, so a wrong path looked exactly like a
18:#     total behavioural failure. need_file() aborts the gate instead.
19:#
20:# Verdicts are PASS / FAIL / SKIP. A SKIP is never counted as a PASS.
21:
22:ROOT=${ROOT:-/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL}
23:IMAGES=$ROOT/_images
24:LOGDIR=${LOGDIR:-/tmp/gxregress}
25:#  d1: EXPORT, or the driver cannot see it.  A plain assignment here reaches the gates'
26:#  own shell redirections but NOT a python child, so drive_guest.py kept writing its two
27:#  pty logs to the hardcoded default while every other artefact relocated -- a mutation
28:#  census then poisoned the shared logs gate 6 grades.  Measured: without this line a
29:#  caller who writes `LOGDIR=x ./run.sh` propagates (env-prefixed vars are exported) but a
30:#  caller who writes `LOGDIR=x; ./run.sh` does not, so the defect was CONDITIONALLY silent.
31:export LOGDIR
32:mkdir -p "$LOGDIR"
33:
34:#  #426: classify a rig's reported budget.  IT LIVES HERE, NOT IN THE GATE, so the branch
35:#  logic can be exercised offline by a selftest instead of only under a weekly rig boot.
36:#  Echoes ONE DISTINGUISHABLE TOKEN per case:
37:#    absent      the rig deliberately has no budget (landisk: its count nearly halves under
38:#                host load, so any ceiling would be invented rather than measured)
39:#    calibrated  a value inside the range measured for that rig
40:#    zeroed      ZERO ON A RIG THAT IS CALIBRATED -- the case that used to print a green
41:#                "deliberately absent".  MEASURED: a luna88k run reporting BUDGET=0 passed
42:#                11/11 while the row said something false about a 12 G rig, and the driver's
43:#                own ceiling went falsy at the same time.  RED, not a skip: the gate HOLDS the
44:#                calibration, so a skip would claim an ignorance it does not have.
45:#    out-of-range / unreported  as they say.
46:budget_class() {
47:    #  ${2-} because every gate here runs under `set -u`, where a one-argument call would
48:    #  abort the gate with "$2: unbound variable" instead of classifying.  A classifier that
49:    #  kills its caller on a malformed call is worse than one that says `unreported`.
50:    case "${1-}:${2-}" in
51:    landisk:0)   echo absent ;;
52:    luna88k:0)   echo zeroed ;;
53:    *:)          echo unreported ;;
54:    luna88k:*)   if [ "$2" -ge 8174849291 ] 2>/dev/null && [ "$2" -le 15571141508 ]
55:                 then echo calibrated; else echo out-of-range; fi ;;
56:    *:0)         echo absent ;;
57:    *)           echo uncalibrated-rig ;;
58:    esac
59:}
60:
61:C_OK=$'\033[32m'; C_BAD=$'\033[31m'; C_SKIP=$'\033[33m'; C_OFF=$'\033[0m'
62:[ -t 1 ] || { C_OK=""; C_BAD=""; C_SKIP=""; C_OFF=""; }
63:
64:# Exit codes. SKIP is 77, NOT 2, because bash exits 2 on a SYNTAX ERROR -- so with 2 as
65:# the skip code a typo in any gate was silently downgraded to "coverage gap" and run.sh
66:# still exited 0. Anything non-zero that is not 77 is a failure.
67:EXIT_SKIP=77
68:
69:GATE_NAME=""
70:_fails=0
71:_checks=0
72:
73:gate_begin() { GATE_NAME=$1; _fails=0; _checks=0
74:    echo "############################################################"
75:    echo "## GATE: $GATE_NAME"
76:    echo "############################################################"
77:}
78:
79:# check <description> <actual> <expected>
80:check() {
81:    _checks=$((_checks+1))
82:    if [ "$2" = "$3" ]; then
83:        printf "  ${C_OK}ok${C_OFF}   %-52s %s\n" "$1" "$2"
84:    else
85:        printf "  ${C_BAD}FAIL${C_OFF} %-52s got=%s want=%s\n" "$1" "$2" "$3"
86:        _fails=$((_fails+1))
87:    fi
88:}
89:
90:# check_min <description> <actual> <minimum>
91:check_min() {
92:    _checks=$((_checks+1))
93:    if [ "$2" -ge "$3" ] 2>/dev/null; then
94:        printf "  ${C_OK}ok${C_OFF}   %-52s %s (>= %s)\n" "$1" "$2" "$3"
95:    else
96:        printf "  ${C_BAD}FAIL${C_OFF} %-52s got=%s want>=%s\n" "$1" "$2" "$3"
97:        _fails=$((_fails+1))
98:    fi
99:}
100:
101:note() { printf "  --   %s\n" "$*"; }
102:
103:# A gate may declare that part of its coverage did not run (a rig whose image is absent,
104:# say). That must not be absorbed into a PASS: it downgrades the whole gate to SKIP, so a
105:# missing input can never be reported as evidence of correctness.
106:_degraded=0
107:degrade() { _degraded=1; printf "  ${C_SKIP}skip${C_OFF} %s\n" "$*"; }
108:
109:gate_end() {
110:    echo
111:    if [ "$_checks" = 0 ]; then
112:        echo "${C_SKIP}$GATE_NAME: SKIP${C_OFF} (no checks ran)"; return $EXIT_SKIP
113:    elif [ "$_fails" != 0 ]; then
114:        echo "${C_BAD}$GATE_NAME: FAIL${C_OFF} ($_fails of $_checks checks)"; return 1
115:    elif [ "$_degraded" != 0 ]; then
116:        echo "${C_SKIP}$GATE_NAME: SKIP${C_OFF} ($_checks checks passed, but part of the gate could not run)"
117:        return $EXIT_SKIP
118:    else
119:        echo "${C_OK}$GATE_NAME: PASS${C_OFF} ($_checks checks)"; return 0
120:    fi
121:}
122:
123:#  #371: gate_skip() exits with the SKIP code, which run.sh maps to
124:#  REGRESS_PASS_WITH_GAPS -- a green-ish verdict. It never consulted the running
125:#  failure counter, so a gate that had ALREADY recorded red checks and then hit a
126:#  gate_skip (a probe that produced no result line, a missing rig image) reported
127:#  those reds as a skip and they vanished from the battery. Measured on a
128:#  synthetic gate: one FAIL then gate_skip exited 77, PASS_WITH_GAPS. This is the
129:#  same "a green row means nothing" class the gate rows themselves guard against,
130:#  in the harness's own control flow.
131:#
132:#  So a gate_skip AFTER failures is a FAIL, not a skip: the skip cannot un-record
133:#  what already failed. A gate_skip with a clean slate stays a genuine skip (a
134:#  preflight that could not run at all -- need_file/need_exec). degrade() was
135:#  already correct: gate_end() tests _fails before _degraded, so a degraded gate
136:#  with recorded failures already reports FAIL. Nothing there to change.
137:gate_skip() {
138:    echo
139:    if [ "${_fails:-0}" != 0 ]; then
140:        echo "${C_BAD}$GATE_NAME: FAIL${C_OFF} ($_fails of $_checks checks failed" \
141:             "before this section could not run: $*)"
142:        exit 1
143:    fi
144:    echo "${C_SKIP}$GATE_NAME: SKIP${C_OFF} -- $*"; exit $EXIT_SKIP
145:}
146:
147:need_file() {
148:    for f in "$@"; do
149:        [ -e "$f" ] || gate_skip "missing required input: $f"
150:    done
151:}
152:
153:need_exec() {
154:    for f in "$@"; do
155:        [ -x "$f" ] || gate_skip "missing or non-executable binary: $f"
156:    done
157:}
158:
159:# run_emu <seconds> <logfile> <binary> [args...]
160:# Always unbuffered, always with stdin closed so a guest prompt cannot wedge the run.
161:run_emu() {
162:    local secs=$1 log=$2; shift 2
163:    ( cd "$IMAGES" 2>/dev/null || cd /; timeout "$secs" stdbuf -o0 -e0 "$@" </dev/null ) \
164:        > "$log" 2>&1
165:    return 0
166:}
167:
168:# run_emu_progress <backstop_s> <budget_instrs> <stall_s> <marker_re> <logfile> <binary> [args...]
169:#
170:# #419: run_emu()'s wall-clock budget is a LOAD-SENSITIVE ORACLE. Under host load the guest
171:# makes less progress in the same seconds, the marker never appears, and the row FAILs
172:# INDISTINGUISHABLY FROM A REAL CAPABILITY REGRESSION -- measured twice, from panel seats and
173:# from interactive greps. This runs the emulator against a budget of GUEST WORK instead, and
174:# -- the part that actually fixes the defect -- it REPORTS WHY IT STOPPED.
175:#
176:# Echoes one line: "reason=<MARKER|BUDGET|STALLED|BACKSTOP|EXITED|ABSENT> ninstrs=<N> wall=<S>"
177:#
178:#   MARKER    the regex matched. Stop AT ONCE: today's run burns its whole budget AFTER
179:#             login because the guest never exits, so this is also where the speed-up is.
180:#   BUDGET    the guest executed <budget_instrs> and still never matched. It was given a full
181:#             allowance of WORK, not of host seconds, so LOAD CANNOT EXPLAIN IT -> the caller
182:#             may score it FAIL with confidence.
183:#   STALLED   no instruction progress for <stall_s>. A slow host still executes instructions;
184:#             a WEDGED guest does not. Not universal -- a BUSY-LOOP hang executes for ever and
185:#             keeps emitting records, so this detects a wedge, not every hang. It is what makes
186:#             a wedged boot DISTINGUISHABLE from load,
187:#             and it is upstream 0.7.0's exact signature on luna88k.
188:#   BACKSTOP  wall clock expired while instructions were still advancing. The only genuinely
189:#             load-ambiguous outcome, and the only one a caller may treat as inconclusive.
190:#   EXITED    the emulator ended under its own power -- exited or crashed -- rather than
191:#             being stopped. A CRASH IS NOT A TIMEOUT, and reporting one as BACKSTOP would
192:#             put a dead build into the single reason a caller may excuse as load.
193:#   ABSENT    no -N record ever appeared. A HARNESS FAULT, and it must be scored RED: drop
194:#             `-N` from the args, or break the extraction by one character, and every leg
195:#             would otherwise fall to BACKSTOP for ever while the battery stayed green and
196:#             the capability rows tested nothing.
197:#
198:# *** DELIBERATELY POLLS A FILE INSTEAD OF PIPING. Rule 1 at the top of this file exists
199:# because a lost 4 KB block once scored a good boot as ZERO and it was reported as a
200:# capability regression. `stdbuf -o0` fixes GXEMUL's buffering, but a `gxemul | grep` reader
201:# block-buffers ITS OWN stdout and re-creates the identical bug one process later. Worse, a
202:# SIGPIPE-based stop only fires on the emulator's NEXT WRITE -- which never comes for a wedged
203:# guest, precisely the case the stop exists to catch. Polling a file has neither failure mode,
204:# and the log is complete on disk however the run ends.
205:#
206:# IT HAS A THIRD, THOUGH, AND THE FIRST WINDOW SIZE HIT IT: a tail window smaller than the
207:# gap between two -N records loses the oracle entirely and reports ABSENT for a perfectly
208:# healthy guest. See the window comment below. ***
209:#
210:# MEASURED BASIS (eleven luna88k boots, 2026-08-14). Instructions-to-login spans
211:# 7,315,767,413 (loaded) to 7,785,570,754 -- 3.57 % across eight idle runs, 6.42 % across an
212:# 8x host-speed range. Dropping the page cache did NOT widen it. Load moves the count DOWN
213:# ~4 %, so the count is not pure guest work, but it errs in the SAFE direction for a ceiling.
214:#
215:# *** THE MECHANISM FOR THAT ~4 % IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and an earlier version of this comment
216:# asserted one that the source contradicts. It claimed the m88k idle fold credits 8191
217:# instructions per wall-paced usleep(500) iteration. The constant is real
218:# (N_DYNTRANS_IDLE_BREAK = N_SAFE_DYNTRANS_LIMIT = (1<<13)-1 = 8191, added at
219:# cpu_m88k_instr.c:2611 and :2660) but it is SUBTRACTED BACK at cpu_dyntrans.c:377 before
220:# n_instrs is accumulated at :392, so the net credit per fold is ~0, not 8191. The DIRECTION
221:# is measured and the conclusion rests on the measurement; the explanation does not. Do not
222:# re-assert a mechanism here without reading both sites. ***
223:#
224:# The decisive run: under 8 busy loops on 8 cores the guest STILL reached login, at
225:# 7,349,301,148 instructions in 742 s -- the same run that scored 1:1:0 and FAILed at the old
226:# 300 s wall budget. That is 9.9 M instr/s, which is the slowest rate measured here.
227:run_emu_progress() {
228:    local backstop=$1 budget=$2 stall=$3 marker=$4 log=$5; shift 5
229:    local t0 last_n=0 last_change n now reason= pid
230:
231:    : > "$log"
232:    t0=$(date +%s); last_change=$t0
233:    # *** DO NOT WRAP THIS IN A SUBSHELL. `$!` would then be the SUBSHELL's pid, and killing
234:    # it reaps the wrapper while ORPHANING the emulator. Measured on the first gate run of
235:    # #419: the pristine leg was correctly classified STALLED at 120 s, its "kill" removed
236:    # only the subshell, and the emulator was still burning 99.9 % of a core four minutes
237:    # later while the next two legs measured themselves under that self-inflicted load.
238:    # Starting `timeout` directly makes `$!` the process we actually need to signal. ***
239:    local oldpwd=$PWD
240:    cd "$IMAGES" 2>/dev/null || cd /
241:    timeout "$backstop" stdbuf -o0 -e0 "$@" </dev/null > "$log" 2>&1 &
242:    pid=$!
243:    cd "$oldpwd" || true
244:
245:    while kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null; do
246:        # tail -c keeps this O(1) in log size rather than O(n) per poll.
247:        #
248:        # *** THE WINDOW WAS 2048 AND THAT WAS TOO SMALL -- measured, not reasoned.
249:        # A guest emitting one -N record per 2 s with ~6 KB of console output between
250:        # records scored ABSENT with instrs=0: a healthy executing build reported as a
251:        # harness fault AND a capability regression, which is exactly the misclassification
252:        # this function exists to remove.  On the real luna88k log the max inter-record gap
253:        # is 2334 bytes -- ALREADY LARGER THAN THE OLD WINDOW.  It did not bite only because
254:        # the miss must persist for the whole stall period.  64 KB is ~28x the observed
255:        # worst gap and still O(1) per poll. ***
256:        n=$(tail -c 65536 "$log" 2>/dev/null | grep -ao '\[ *[0-9]\+ instrs' | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9')
257:        n=${n:-$last_n}
258:        now=$(date +%s)
259:        [ "$n" != "$last_n" ] && { last_n=$n; last_change=$now; }
260:        if grep -aq "$marker" "$log" 2>/dev/null; then reason=MARKER; break; fi
261:        if [ "${n:-0}" -gt "$budget" ] 2>/dev/null;  then reason=BUDGET; break; fi
262:        if [ $((now - last_change)) -ge "$stall" ];  then reason=STALLED; break; fi
263:        sleep 1
264:    done
265:
266:    # The emulator outlives the poll loop unless it is killed here, and killing `timeout`
267:    # alone is not enough to rely on: reap its children explicitly by pid. Do NOT use
268:    # `pkill -f <pattern>` for this -- the pattern appears in the invoking shell's own
269:    # command line, so pkill SIGTERMs itself before finishing the job (measured: it exited
270:    # 15 having killed nothing).
271:    local kid exitst=0
272:    if [ -n "$reason" ]; then
273:        # We stopped it deliberately, so its exit status carries no information.
274:        for kid in $(pgrep -P "$pid" 2>/dev/null); do kill -TERM "$kid" 2>/dev/null; done
275:        kill -TERM "$pid" 2>/dev/null
276:        sleep 1
277:        for kid in $(pgrep -P "$pid" 2>/dev/null); do kill -9 "$kid" 2>/dev/null; done
278:        kill -9 "$pid" 2>/dev/null
279:        wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null
280:    else
281:        # The poll loop ended because the process is GONE. Collect its status before
282:        # touching it: that status is the only thing that distinguishes `timeout`
283:        # firing (124) from the emulator exiting or crashing under its own power.
284:        wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null; exitst=$?
285:    fi
286:
287:    if [ -z "$reason" ]; then
288:        # *** RE-CHECK THE LOG BEFORE DEFAULTING TO BACKSTOP.  Measured 3/3,
289:        # deterministic: the poll period is ~1006 ms, so a marker landing in the final
290:        # poll window was scored BACKSTOP even though the guest had reached it -- the
291:        # caller then called a SUCCESSFUL BOOT a regression while the marker-count row
292:        # simultaneously passed on 1:1:1.  Two contradictory rows from one run. ***
293:        if grep -aq "$marker" "$log" 2>/dev/null; then
294:            reason=MARKER
295:        elif [ "$exitst" != 124 ]; then
296:            # *** A CRASH IS NOT A TIMEOUT.  The loop also ends when the emulator DIES,
297:            # and reporting that as BACKSTOP puts a crashed build into the one reason a
298:            # caller may treat as load-ambiguous.  timeout(1) returns 124 if and only if
299:            # IT fired; anything else means the emulator ended on its own.  Measured on
300:            # the shipped code: a fake emitting one record then `exit 3` returned
301:            # reason=BACKSTOP ninstrs=100000000 wall=2. ***
302:            reason=EXITED
303:        else
304:            reason=BACKSTOP
305:        fi
306:        # Same race on the count: a run that finishes inside one poll interval has a
307:        # valid record on disk that no poll ever observed.
308:        n=$(tail -c 65536 "$log" 2>/dev/null | grep -ao '\[ *[0-9]\+ instrs' | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9')
309:        [ -n "$n" ] && [ "${n:-0}" -gt "${last_n:-0}" ] 2>/dev/null && last_n=$n
310:    fi
311:    # An absent oracle stream outranks most other reasons: if -N produced nothing then
312:    # nothing was measured, whatever else the run appeared to do.  It does NOT outrank
313:    # EXITED -- a build that died is a defect in its own right, and the caller's
314:    # instruction-count row catches a dropped `-N` independently of the reason.
315:    [ "${last_n:-0}" -eq 0 ] && [ "$reason" != MARKER ] && [ "$reason" != EXITED ] && reason=ABSENT
316:
317:    echo "reason=$reason ninstrs=$last_n wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))"
318:}
319:
320:# count <logfile> <pattern>   -- number of matching lines, 0 if the file is absent.
321:#
322:# NOT `[ -f x ] && grep -ac ... || echo 0`. On zero matches grep prints "0" AND exits 1,
323:# so the || arm fires too and the function emits TWO lines ("0\n0"). Measured: 3 bytes,
324:# 2 lines. That silently corrupted every marker string built from it, and made gate 6's
325:# one deliberate expected-negative assertion impossible to satisfy.
326:count() {
327:    [ -f "$1" ] || { echo 0; return; }
328:    local c
329:    c=$(grep -ac -- "$2" "$1" 2>/dev/null)
330:    echo "${c:-0}"
331:}
1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:"""#549 kill table: every enumerated edit of the FIXED dev_fbctrl.c, each a text transformation with exactly one match
3:(a find-text that matches nothing is a FAIL, so each mutant also pins the presence of what it edits).
4:usage: python mutants549.py <fixed dev_fbctrl.c> <outdir>   -> <outdir>/<name>.c for every mutant"""
5:import io
6:import os
7:import sys
8:
9:PRED = "else if (idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {"
10:LATCH_TEST = "if (!d->warned_port) {"
11:LATCH_SET = "\t\t\t\td->warned_port = 1;\n"
12:ACCEPT = "\t\t} else\n\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n"
13:REFUSE_OPEN = PRED + "\n"
14:ANNOUNCE = "\t\t\t\tfatal(\"[ fbctrl: port number 0x%llx is out of range; \"\n"
15:HEAD_PORT = ("\t\telse {\n"
16:             "\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n"
17:             "\t\t\tif (idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS)\n"
18:             "\t\t\t\tfatal(\"[ WARNING: fbctrl port number is out\"\n"
19:             "\t\t\t\t    \" of range! ]\\n\");\n"
20:             "\t\t}\n")
21:DATA_GUARD = ("\t\tif (d->current_port < 0 || d->current_port\n"
22:              "\t\t    >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {\n"
23:              "\t\t\tfatal(\"[ fbctrl port number is out of range! ]\\n\");\n"
24:              "\t\t\texit(1);\n"
25:              "\t\t}\n")
26:
27:#  (name, class, [(old, new), ...])  -- class: FLOOR-b | d (the fix's own predicate/store/latch) | e (disclosed)
28:MUTANTS = [
29:    ("M_revert", "FLOOR-b: HEAD's PORT arm verbatim (store, then warn)", None),
30:    ("M_gt", "d: >= becomes >", [(PRED, "else if (idata > DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
31:    ("M_u32", "d: compare on (uint32_t)idata", [(PRED, "else if ((uint32_t) idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
32:    ("M_i32", "d: compare on (int)idata", [(PRED, "else if ((int) idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
33:    ("M_i64", "d: compare on (int64_t)idata", [(PRED, "else if ((int64_t) idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
34:    ("M_decl", "d: idata declared int64_t", [("\tuint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;", "\tint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;")]),
35:    ("M_mod33", "d: compare on idata % 2^33", [(PRED, "else if (idata % 0x200000000ULL >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
36:    ("M_bit", "d: a bit test for the bound", [(PRED, "else if (idata & 0x100000008ULL) {")]),
37:    ("M_wide", "d: refusal only for writes of 4+ bytes", [(PRED, "else if (len >= 4 && idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
38:    ("M_acc4", "d: accept only 4-byte writes", [(ACCEPT, "\t\t} else if (len == 4)\n\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n")]),
39:    ("M_rej2", "d: legal selector 2 never accepted", [(ACCEPT, "\t\t} else if (idata != 2)\n\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n")]),
40:    ("M_and5", "d: the refusal rewrites the previous selector (&= 5)",
41:     [(REFUSE_OPEN, REFUSE_OPEN + "\t\t\td->current_port &= 5;\n")]),
42:    ("M_reset0", "d: the refusal resets the selector to 0", [(REFUSE_OPEN, REFUSE_OPEN + "\t\t\td->current_port = 0;\n")]),
43:    ("M_clamp", "d: the refusal stores idata & 7", [(REFUSE_OPEN, REFUSE_OPEN + "\t\t\td->current_port = idata & 7;\n")]),
44:    ("M_store", "d: the refusal stores the value anyway", [(REFUSE_OPEN, REFUSE_OPEN + "\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n")]),
45:    ("M_latch0", "d: the latch never set (announce every time)", [(LATCH_SET, "")]),
46:    ("M_latchinv", "d: the latch test inverted (never announce)", [(LATCH_TEST, "if (d->warned_port) {")]),
47:    ("M_latchcnt", "d: the latch counts (announces on the 3rd, 5th, ...)", [(LATCH_TEST, "if (d->warned_port++ == 2) {")]),
48:    ("M_perval", "d: a per-value latch (announce when the value changes)",
49:     [(LATCH_TEST, "if (d->warned_port != (int) idata) {"), (LATCH_SET, "\t\t\t\td->warned_port = (int) idata;\n")]),
50:    ("M_debug", "d: the announce demoted to debug()", [(ANNOUNCE, ANNOUNCE.replace("fatal(", "debug("))]),
51:    ("M_ann_any", "d: the announce fires on ACCEPTED writes too",
52:     [(PRED, "else if (!d->warned_port && (d->warned_port = 1)) {\n\t\t\tfatal(\"[ fbctrl: port number 0x%llx is out of range; \"\n\t\t\t    \"the selector is unchanged (once) ]\\n\", (unsigned long long) idata);\n\t\t\tif (idata < DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) d->current_port = idata;\n\t\t} else if (idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS) {")]),
53:    ("M_reflatch", "d: the refusal only until the first announce (later ones stored)",
54:     [(PRED, "else if (idata >= DEV_FBCTRL_NPORTS && !d->warned_port) {")]),
55:    ("M_acclatch", "d: accepts only until the first refusal",
56:     [(ACCEPT, "\t\t} else if (!d->warned_port)\n\t\t\td->current_port = idata;\n")]),
57:    ("M_static", "e (disclosed): a process-global latch",
58:     [(LATCH_TEST, "static int warned_port_g;\n\t\t\tif (!warned_port_g) {"), (LATCH_SET, "\t\t\t\twarned_port_g = 1;\n")]),
59:    ("M_noguard", "e (disclosed): the DATA arm's range check deleted", [(DATA_GUARD, "")]),
60:]
61:
62:
63:def main():
64:    src, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
65:    os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
66:    base = io.open(src, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
67:    assert "\r\n" not in base
68:    head = io.open(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(src), "dev_fbctrl_HEAD.c"), encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
69:    for name, cls, edits in MUTANTS:
70:        if edits is None:
71:            s = head                                  # verbatim HEAD: the removed defect at the fixed site
72:        else:
73:            s = base
74:            for old, new in edits:
75:                n = s.count(old)
76:                if n != 1:

VERDICT: SHIP WITH CHANGES

1. **[P2] The refusal contract has an undisclosed state gap.** A one-statement edit outside table 2—adding `d->current_port |= 1;` inside the rejection branch—would violate “keep the previous selector” while **predictably retaining 41/41**. P observes only previous selectors 5 and 7; K observes 5. Those remain unchanged. F starts at 0 but never reads the resulting selector or DATA. Both F rows therefore remain green despite changing 0 to 1. Add preservation checks from reset and an even selector. This is a static prediction, not a measured mutant. See [session definitions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py:288).

2. **[P2] A one-character probe edit defeats the boundary test without changing the shown pins.** Change the numeric input `8` to `9` in the second `REFUSE` tuple, leaving its label unchanged. The existing `M_gt` mutant would then predictably score **41/41**: the row named “refuse 8 (sw)” actually exercises 9, and nothing else exercises rejection at 8. Instruction-disassembly controls cannot detect that register-value change. Independently check the presence of the boundary input and derive labels from actual test inputs. See [REFUSE](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py:289).

3. **[P2] The added historical explanation is false for wide selectors.** The [device comment](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_fbctrl.c:121) and [gate comment](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_mips_folds.sh:37) say an out-of-range write made the next DATA access terminate. Previously, assignment narrowed `uint64_t idata` to `int`; some out-of-range inputs became valid selectors. Your own [saved measurement](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbctrlportkill_20260924/opus549_measured/runs/sd_row_base.out:1) shows the wide input becoming selector 3 and the DATA read surviving. Say that DATA terminated **when the stored, narrowed selector was outside 0–7**. Make the probe’s historical description equally explicit.

4. **[P2] The process-global-latch exclusion is valid, but its justification is not.** “Every machine installs ONE fbctrl” does not establish one instance per process. Native configuration parsing supports multiple machines through [emul_add_machine](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/emul_parse.c:461), and each ordinary testmips setup installs its own controller. Two native testmips machines could distinguish a global latch without adding a synthetic device. Replace the justification with “these sessions each launch one machine in a fresh process.” The useful missing measurement is two native instances in one process, each announcing its first refusal.

5. **[P2] The parse guard is not a complete record validator.** It rejects absent markers, duplicate exact DONE lines, duplicate RESULT/CONTROL lines with the expected prefixes, malformed sole records, and a nonzero Python exit. However, one valid record plus `FBCTRLP_DONE=broken` passes; a malformed RESULT line lacking `=` is likewise ignored alongside a valid RESULT. Marker order is unchecked. It also accepts just the three success markers without any individual check rows: 41 is self-reported. I confirmed these outcomes with an in-memory model of the shown checks, without running a gate. Validate the reserved marker prefixes and record structure, and reconcile the reported total with emitted rows. See [parse guard](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_mips_folds.sh:56).

6. **Gate 16’s result does change in previously possible states.** The new comment about `gate_skip` is supported by [lib.sh](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lib.sh:137).

   | State | Before | After |
   |---|---|---|
   | Binary missing | SKIP | SKIP |
   | Kernel missing; fbctrl passes | SKIP | SKIP |
   | Kernel missing; fbctrl fails or produces an incomplete record | SKIP | FAIL |
   | Kernel present; fold rows fail | FAIL | FAIL |
   | Kernel present; folds pass; fbctrl fails | PASS | FAIL |

   An early Python death fails either parsing or the exit-status check. Moving the kernel check preserves failures as intended; it does not preserve every previous verdict.

7. **[P2] Re-selection does not justify “every row fails only on its own value,” and controls can overstate completed coverage.** Register values are collected only after the entire session survives. A later death therefore discards earlier observations and can turn earlier rows red. The saved [M_acc4 output](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbctrlportkill_20260924/table2/M_acc4.raw:1) demonstrates this: A dies after 5/38 steps, all acceptance rows fail—including selector 0 written with an accepted `sw`—yet its “disassembly of every stepped word” control and overall CONTROL remain green. Re-selection removes the particular stale-selector cascade; it does not make observations independent. Qualify the contract and distinguish checks of completed steps from full-session coverage.

8. **[P3] Tighten the remaining NOT CLAIMED wording.** `warned_port` follows the eight-element array, but dereferencing `port[8]` is undefined behavior; “would land on the latch itself” needs an explicit ABI/layout qualification. The kept DATA guard is indeed unreachable under the current initialization and PORT assignment invariant. The two other direct exits remain present, including the reset-to-COMMAND route. Describe command classification in terms of the stored `int`, and offset classification within the registered device window. Also disclose that acceptance uses selected width/selector combinations, not their full cross-product, and that F does not observe selector preservation. These limitations belong alongside the two disclosed mutation escapes. See [contract](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbctrl_port_probe.py:29).

9. **[P2] Additional records are owed.** Update the existing `gatedevicesplit` debt to include gate 16 and this probe; the new comment explicitly invokes that obligation. Record the surviving mutations and scope limitations above, and preserve the final reviewed probe with corresponding evidence if it changes. The command/offset filings need actual identifiers before the committed text says “Those are filed.” Preserve the disclosed fresh-export hygiene discrepancy—71 versus the proposed 74 pin—as an inherited, owner-accepted limitation; the working-tree gate pass does not validate that export.

I read the full brief and checked the permitted source and saved evidence. I ran no builds, detectors, gates, or battery. Build freshness, `est/` identity, the final staged hunks, and unwritten records remain independently unverified.
